idea: Per-seat config (generic seat-level key=value registry with descriptions, values, defaults and write tiers; `ht config seat` read/list for every caller, agent-writable low-risk keys such as nudge threshold, `ht human config seat write` for gated keys such as queue wake, replacing the node-level programmatic_wake setting with migration), Codex /side queue wake routing to the owed root session by exact UUID (spikes and qualification), and removing the /side placeholder from Codex's empty-composer list so native wake defers instead of typing into a side chat.

cluster dropped: queue-precheck — in `QueueWakeRuntime::attempt`, before `prepare_wake_intent` or any helper spawn, add a cheap root-scoped pre-check (root hook TurnState busy, or a host status or composer state the arm would refuse) that returns `QueueDispatch::Refused` with a detail string ("root turn busy", and so on). - finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"roast-code/round-1","artifact":"docs/superpowers/runs/2026-11-26-per-seat-config-side-wake/2026-11-27-per-seat-config-side-wake-roast-pr-1.md","kind":"roast","verdict":"Should-fix (10 confirmed)","occurrences":[{"section":"Confirmed findings            ← consumed by super-design, one task per finding","position":1,"key":"[Should-fix] scripts/wake-e2e.py:2979","text":"[Should-fix] scripts/wake-e2e.py:2979 — The live cases `side-queue/group` and `side-native-defer/group` have only mocked selftest coverage and no recorded run against a real Codex. Queue routing to the root beside a /side session has no end-to-end evidence through the product path. [lanes: testing]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute REJECT, dissent / ground ✓)\n  evidence: run.md root-acceptance row `live-qualification` is `requiredLive: true` (ht-z41e.10) with `\"evidence\": []`. The run's evidence directory holds only leak-check.txt, run-2, run-3 and side_spike.py; the spike used raw `codex queue` with no daemon, so selection, prepare and arm were never exercised. Final review lines 42, 53, 59: the two cases \"have never run against a real Codex\"; listed under \"Must fix before landing\". Refute dissent: this is a tracked pending gate, not a code defect. Both confirming seats agree it is tracked; the row is still empty.\n  fix-shape hint: run ht-z41e.10 at the final SHA (wake-e2e Codex `--queue-proof` matrix plus side-queue, side-native-defer, side-nudge, side-draft) and record the evidence directory in the row. This spends real Codex quota."},{"section":"Confirmed findings            ← consumed by super-design, one task per finding","position":2,"key":"[Should-fix] tests/integration/herdr_release_smoke.rs:1175","text":"[Should-fix] tests/integration/herdr_release_smoke.rs:1175 — The inverted /side expectation in the ignored `herdr_release_wake` test and the new Ctrl-C side-close branch in the codex.js stand-in have never been executed. The only real-host check of \"native wake defers on a side chat, then wakes after close\" has no run evidence. [lanes: testing]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute REJECT, dissent / ground ✓)\n  evidence: the test is `#[ignore]` and needs a private Herdr. .superpowers/sdd/ht-z41e-plan/progress.md:18 records Task 1 as \"[unexecuted-test] ... compiled by clippy but not run\". Final review line 52 lists running it on the final SHA under \"Must fix before landing\": \"neither has ever executed\". Refute dissent: running it is a per-release gate already tracked. The confirming seats agree it is tracked; no run exists on the branch.\n  fix-shape hint: run `herdr_release_wake` once on the final SHA through scripts/herdr-release-smoke and keep the output with the run evidence."},{"section":"Confirmed findings            ← consumed by super-design, one task per finding","position":3,"key":"[Should-fix] src/store/wake.rs:272","text":"[Should-fix] src/store/wake.rs:272 — No test checks that the production store fills the attention witness's `programmatic_wake` field. Removing `.with_programmatic_wake(...)` from `load_candidate` leaves every test green and silently disables the scheduler's \"flag flip lifts unreceived suppression\" logic. [lanes: testing]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `with_programmatic_wake` is called only at src/store/wake.rs:272 (production) and tests/scheduler/wake_receipt.rs:62 (a `LadderStore` fake). The field defaults to false (src/ports.rs:3005). The scheduler reads it at src/scheduler/mod.rs:1007 and :1117. No test in tests/ loads a store candidate and asserts `attention_witness.programmatic_wake()`. Queue selection is unaffected (selection.rs:172 re-reads the flag); the exposed behaviour is the flip-lifts-suppression path (ht-z41e.4).\n  fix-shape hint: one store-level test: set `seat_config programmatic_wake=on`, load the candidate, assert the witness flag is true, and false when off."},{"section":"Confirmed findings            ← consumed by super-design, one task per finding","position":4,"key":"[Should-fix] src/scheduler/mod.rs:1012","text":"[Should-fix] src/scheduler/mod.rs:1012 — The closed-loop receipt check is seat-scoped, so a /side binding's daemon contact counts as receipt of a queued root wake. The design's R5 rule (only root contact settles it) is neither implemented nor tested on this path. [lanes: concurrency-async, testing]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: design §2 Arrival (design.md:187): \"only contact from the root binding counts as receipt of the queued wake (R5); a side binding's contact never settles it.\" src/scheduler/mod.rs:1012 decides `silent` with `self.hook_pull.contacted_since(&seat, awaiting.since) == Some(false)`. `contacts` is keyed by seat (src/service/delivery_pull.rs:180-189). `record_contact(&claim.seat, ..)` runs for any binding (src/service/dispatch.rs:2138-2141). The design test plan (line 251) lists \"side contact not arrival\"; no such test exists. Impact bound stated by all seats: intent fulfillment stays root-exact; the loss is the `wake=unreceived` diagnostic plus one extra ladder retry, not a lost wake.\n  severity: seats rated Nit / Should-fix / Nit. Held at Should-fix because the profile is production and this is a stated design rule whose listed test is absent.\n  fix-shape hint: for a queued-wake `AwaitingReceipt`, count only contact from the root binding (per-binding or per-native-session contact time) and add the \"side contact is not arrival\" test; or amend R5 and TRUST-POLICY to accept seat-scoped contact."},{"section":"Confirmed findings            ← consumed by super-design, one task per finding","position":5,"key":"[Nit] src/harness/codex.rs:1525","text":"[Nit] src/harness/codex.rs:1525 — Root selection treats only bindings without the persisted `concurrent_session` fork marker as roots; the spike's marker (`transcript_path`, present on every event) is not used. A /side binding enrolled without the fork marker makes the process name no root, the seat drops to native, and native is refused while the side is displayed, so an opted-in seat gets no wake until the side closes. [lanes: premortem, correctness, testing]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute REJECT, dissent / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `wake_process_root` filters `!c.concurrent_session` and returns None for zero or two-plus roots. `concurrent_session=1` is set only when `request.fork` (src/store/bindings.rs:1650, :1757). `transcript_path` is decoded but never persisted. spike-side-identity.md follow-up: \"Confirm the Codex adapter persists `transcript_path` nullness (or `fork`)\". The result is fail-closed and equals queue-off behaviour. How often a side binding lacks its fork SessionStart is not established. Refute dissent: documented tradeoff in the adapter doc comment; the final review lists it as `marker-deviation` under \"Deferred OK\".\n  fix-shape hint: persist a null `transcript_path` as the concurrent marker on the binding, on any event. The same change addresses the TRUST-POLICY entry, the test-gap entry and the /fork escalation below."},{"section":"Confirmed findings            ← consumed by super-design, one task per finding","position":6,"key":"[Nit] src/store/seat_config.rs:489; src/store/seat_config.rs:462","text":"[Nit] src/store/seat_config.rs:489; src/store/seat_config.rs:462 — The human-tier operator write checks only that the seat exists locally, not that it is unretired. [lanes: correctness, concurrency-async, data-migrations]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `operator_write` runs `require_local_seat_setting` (src/store/seats/cooperative.rs:211, home-node check only) and `SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM seats WHERE id=?1 AND instance_id=?2)`. Final retirement keeps the seat row with `state='retired'` (src/store/control/retirement.rs:285) and calls `delete_for_final_retirement` once (:311, :338). `seats_with_value` (seat_config.rs:120-129) has no state filter. No test writes to a retired seat. Not run; code reading only. Effect: one orphan row per key and a misleading doctor line.\n  fix-shape hint: refuse the operator write when the seat's retirement is final (a reversibly retired seat keeps its rows, so a write there stays legitimate); add a test."},{"section":"Confirmed findings            ← consumed by super-design, one task per finding","position":7,"key":"[Nit] TRUST-POLICY.md:4277","text":"[Nit] TRUST-POLICY.md:4277 — The new queue-wake-target section says a mis-enrolled side session always falls back to native, \"fail closed, never a wrong target\". `process_root` makes a lone connected binding with `concurrent_session` false its own target, so a side first enrolled by a later event, with the root binding gone, is queue-targeted. That case is not recorded as an accepted limit. [lanes: hygiene-docs]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: TRUST-POLICY.md:4276-4277 quoted text. src/store/wake_intents/selection.rs:37: `[only] => (!only.concurrent).then(|| (only, Vec::new()))`. The fallback holds only when the real root binding is also connected. AGENTS.md: \"A change that weakens an invariant or adds an accepted limit updates TRUST-POLICY.md in the same commit.\" The run's bounded-draft review (lines 31, 32, 68) names the case as PLAUSIBLE. [lanes: test-causality-lifecycle, test-suite-cost]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce REJECT, dissent / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `side_defer_case` (lines 3028-3085) records `checks[\"wake_state_while_side\"]` but `side_defer_passed(setup_ok, typed_while_side, woke, delivered)` (line 327) takes no refused-attempt input. Reproduce dissent: a late daemon is a different fault from the regression under test. A `/side` binding owes every item sent while it is open (shared membership, its own cursor; all bindings when an invitation, receipt or warning is pending), so the reservation has two targets, the queue is never tried, and the wake defers natively until the side closes. Fix: let the queue be tried when every target belongs to one harness process (same harness and fingerprint pid, as `ComposerChoice::Process`), and add a test through the real dispatcher with root and side targets; the runtime and scheduler tests bypass this gate.","text":"B8 not delivered: `src/notification/dispatch.rs:701` keeps `use_queue` only when `targets.len() == 1`, but `cooperative_authority`/`wake_target_rows` (`src/store/wake.rs`) give one target per connected top-level binding that owes something. - finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"inv-46257405-8bd2-418b-ad46-e725034ed0ba/final","artifact":"reviews/inv-46257405-8bd2-418b-ad46-e725034ed0ba/final-review-inv-46257405-8bd2-418b-ad46-e725034ed0ba.md","kind":"final-review","verdict":"not ready (unreviewed: whole-epic coverage and omission provenance remain incomplete)","occurrences":[{"section":"Concerns","position":1,"key":"Busy side turn vs queue guard: `check_programmatic_wake` reuses the native status gate, and the native.rs test `check_programmatic_wake_refuses_a_side_draft_and_unreadable` asserts that a pane with agent status `working` refuses the queue wake. AGENTS.md (side cases paragraph), TRUST-POLICY and the design say instead that a busy side binding does not refuse the root (spike S2) and that under `--queue-proof` a hooked recipient is queue-woken \"even while a side turn is busy\". If Herdr reports the pane `working` while a side turn runs, the root wake waits for the side turn to end, and the `side-nudge`/`side-draft` queue-mode expectations may fail live. Verify in ht-z41e.10, then fix either the docs or the expectation. (PLAUSIBLE, not reproduced.)","text":"Busy side turn vs queue guard: `check_programmatic_wake` reuses the native status gate, and the native.rs test `check_programmatic_wake_refuses_a_side_draft_and_unreadable` asserts that a pane with agent status `working` refuses the queue wake. AGENTS.md (side cases paragraph), TRUST-POLICY and the design say instead that a busy side binding does not refuse the root (spike S2) and that under `--queue-proof` a hooked recipient is queue-woken \"even while a side turn is busy\". If Herdr reports the pane `working` while a side turn runs, the root wake waits for the side turn to end, and the `side-nudge`/`side-draft` queue-mode expectations may fail live. Verify in ht-z41e.10, then fix either the docs or the expectation. (PLAUSIBLE, not reproduced.)"},{"section":"Concerns","position":2,"key":"`side-draft` under `--queue-proof`: the queue guard admits only the exact side placeholder text, so a draft typed into the side composer refuses the queue wake as well. The AGENTS.md text \"side-draft then needs only the intact draft and the delivered item\" holds only if the draft sits somewhere other than the displayed side composer. The new comment says the composer read inside `observed_ui` already resets the window, but `observed_ui` returns `Unknown` without any read when the pane has no agent kind, an unrecognized kind, or no composer policy. In those cases an idle/done observation no longer resets the native focus-dwell window. Example: a focused Codex pane starts its dwell, then Herdr briefly reports no agent at t=30s. At t=61s the first empty read admits typing, although the composer was not observed empty throughout. This contradicts design §Unchanged behavior, which keeps the native focus dwell as it was. The fix is one line: restore the clause.","text":"[minor] `src/host/native.rs:2534` (`observe_target_with_composer`): `reset_empty` lost the base clause `|| (composer_ui && ui != HostUiState::Idle)`. The new comment says the composer read inside `observed_ui` already resets the window, but `observed_ui` returns `Unknown` without any read when the pane has no agent kind, an unrecognized kind, or no composer policy. In those cases an idle/done observation no longer resets the native focus-dwell window. Example: a focused Codex pane starts its dwell, then Herdr briefly reports no agent at t=30s. At t=61s the first empty read admits typing, although the composer was not observed empty throughout. This contradicts design §Unchanged behavior, which keeps the native focus dwell as it was. The fix is one line: restore the clause."},{"section":"Findings","position":2,"key":"[minor] `src/service/delivery_pull.rs` `session_contacts` is never pruned. It gains one entry per (seat, native session) that makes a claim-bearing call over the daemon's lifetime, so memory grows slowly with session churn.","text":"[minor] `src/service/delivery_pull.rs` `session_contacts` is never pruned. It gains one entry per (seat, native session) that makes a claim-bearing call over the daemon's lifetime, so memory grows slowly with session churn."},{"section":"Findings","position":3,"key":"[minor] `src/notification/queue.rs` `ROOTS_MAX`: when 256 roots are retained, the whole map is cleared. This is diagnostic loss only.","text":"[minor] `src/notification/queue.rs` `ROOTS_MAX`: when 256 roots are retained, the whole map is cleared. This is diagnostic loss only."},{"section":"Findings","position":4,"key":"[nit] `src/protocol/commands.rs` places `use crate::protocol::seat_config::ConfigTier;` between `enum PermitMutation` and its `TryFrom` impl.","text":"[nit] `src/protocol/commands.rs` places `use crate::protocol::seat_config::ConfigTier;` between `enum PermitMutation` and its `TryFrom` impl."},{"section":"Concerns","position":1,"key":"The human tier is enforced only by the harness permission policy.
